The VisaVerse AI Hackathon is a global innovation event that brings together creators, developers, researchers, and problem solvers to build AI driven solutions that make the world more connected and accessible. This hackathon focuses on global mobility, international collaboration, and the power of artificial intelligence to remove barriers that limit opportunity.

VisaVerse invites participants from every country to work together in an open, inclusive environment where ideas can cross borders freely. The event is designed to encourage creative thinking, high impact problem solving, and the development of tools that help people move, work, communicate, and collaborate across the world with greater ease.Rules 1. Eligibility
The hackathon is open to participants from any country.

Participants may join individually or as part of a team.

There is no limit on team size unless specified by the organizers.

Organizers, judges, and sponsors may not compete for prizes.

2. Original Work Only
All submissions must be created during the hackathon period.

Pre-existing projects, code, or prototypes may be used only if clearly referenced and significantly expanded during the event.

Plagiarism or uncredited work is not allowed.

3. Use of Artificial Intelligence
Projects must include AI or machine learning as a meaningful component.

Publicly available AI tools, APIs, and models may be used as long as they comply with their respective licenses and terms.

4. Fair Participation
All participants must follow ethical AI practices and avoid harmful or unsafe use cases.

Attacks on other teams, systems, or platforms are strictly prohibited.

Respectful and inclusive behavior is expected throughout the event.

5. Submission Requirements
Participants must submit all required materials before the deadline, including:

Project description

Demo video

Code repository

Technical documentation

Team details

Late submissions may not be accepted.

6. Judging Criteria
Projects will be evaluated based on:

Innovation and creativity

Use of artificial intelligence

Technical quality

Real world impact and relevance

User experience and design

Clarity of presentation and documentation

Judges’ decisions are final.

7. Allowed Tools and Resources
Participants may use:

Any programming language or framework

Open source libraries

Public APIs and datasets

Pre trained AI models, as long as they follow valid usage rights

8. Prohibited Actions
Submitting harmful, unsafe, or illegal content

Using copyrighted materials without permission

Harassing or disrupting other participants

Misrepresenting team identity or work

Using generated work that violates platform or model policies

9. Ownership and Rights
Teams retain ownership of their projects.

The organizers may request permission to feature winning projects in promotional materials, with appropriate credit.

10. Prize Eligibility
To qualify for prizes, teams must meet all submission rules.

Any violation of rules may result in disqualification.

11. Organizer Rights
The organizers reserve the right to update rules, disqualify participants for misconduct, or adjust judging criteria if necessary. 

Participants gain access to expert mentors, technical resources, and a supportive community of innovators. Whether working solo or in a team, every participant has the chance to design solutions that can shape the future of global interaction and digital mobility.

What Makes VisaVerse Special
A truly global event with participation from any location

Challenges centered on real world problems and global access

A strong focus on artificial intelligence and practical innovation

Support from industry mentors and AI specialists

Opportunities for prizes, visibility, and project growth

A diverse community united by curiosity, creativity, and ambition

Challenge Focus Areas
Participants will explore themes such as global mobility, international workforce tools, cultural understanding, AI powered documentation support, borderless collaboration technologies, global fintech, and next generation communication systems.

The goal is simple: build AI driven solutions that help people connect, work, travel, and thrive anywhere in the world.

Requirements
What to Build
Participants are expected to create an AI powered solution that aligns with one of the official VisaVerse AI Hackathon themes. Your project may be a prototype, a working application, a functional demo, or a technical concept supported by proof of feasibility.

Projects should demonstrate creativity, technical depth, and clear value toward improving global mobility, collaboration, or borderless access.

Your build should include:

A functioning prototype or demo that showcases the core features

Clear documentation explaining the idea, problem statement, and implementation

A user focused design that highlights usability and impact

Team or solo projects are welcome.

What to Submit
Participants must submit all required materials before the deadline. Submissions should include:

1. Project Description
A clear explanation of your idea, the problem it addresses, why it matters, and how your solution works.

2. Demo Video
A short video (2 to 5 minutes) showing:

What your project does

How users interact with it

The main features in action
The video does not need to be professionally edited. A screen recording is acceptable.

3. Project Repository or Code
A link to your GitHub or other repository containing:

Source code

Setup instructions

Dependencies

Any additional files needed to run the project

4. Technical Documentation
A brief document or README that includes:

Overview of the solution

Architecture or workflow

AI models or tools used

Instructions on how to run the prototype

5. Team Information
Names, roles, and contact emails of all team members.

Optional Enhancements
UI/UX mockups

Additional datasets or training details

Presentation slides

Prizes
$10,000 in prizes
hackathon Prizes

Why: it sits dead-center in their inspiration list: “intelligent onboarding tools for cross border hiring”, “AI systems that enhance global collaboration in remote teams”, “AI powered compliance automation…” citeturn1view2

No product name. Just domain.

---

## 3) Pain point (real, recurring, not “one-off”)

### Pain point (single sentence)
**Cross-border teams lose weeks because onboarding and compliance are fragmented across HR, legal, IT, payroll, identity, and local rules — and nobody has an end-to-end, continuously updating “readiness picture.”**

### Why it exists
- Data is siloed (HR has contracts, IT has access, Finance has payroll, Legal has policy).  
- Requirements change by country, vendor, and company policy.  
- People discover missing items late (offer accepted → background check → payroll setup → access provisioning → blocked).  
This is not “docs are messy.” It’s **system fragmentation**.

### Current workaround people do today
- HR uses checklists + email threads + spreadsheets  
- Contractors ping managers repeatedly  
- Teams create duplicate “onboarding Notion pages” per region  
- Compliance is handled by expensive specialists or slow internal reviews  
Result: **repeat work + delays + hidden risk**

(These are standard operational practices; no single source, but they’re the dominant real-world workaround patterns in most orgs.)

---

## 4) How our system fixes it (FULL AI, not a wrapper)

### The system we build (name-free)
An **AI-driven “Cross-Border Onboarding & Collaboration Orchestrator”** with **multi-agent reasoning**, not a chatbot.

#### Core AI agents (each must exist)
1) **Requirement Synthesizer Agent**  
   - Takes: destination country, role type, contract type, company policy pack  
   - Outputs: required tasks + required evidence + dependency graph

2) **Evidence Extractor Agent**  
   - Takes: docs/emails/forms (offer letter, passport, proof of address, contract, certificates)  
   - Outputs: structured fields + confidence + missing fields list

3) **Conflict & Risk Agent**  
   - Detects mismatches (start date vs right-to-work doc validity, address mismatch, name variations, contract clause conflicts)  
   - Outputs: “blocking vs non-blocking” issues

4) **Execution Planner Agent**  
   - Builds the optimal order of tasks (what to do first to avoid rework)  
   - Outputs: step plan + who owns each action (HR/IT/worker)

5) **Collaboration Readiness Agent**  
   - Predicts when the worker can actually start contributing (access + equipment + accounts + meetings)  
   - Outputs: readiness date + blockers

This is **AI doing structured reasoning and dependency planning**, not summarizing.

---

## 5) Time & money saved (by party, brutally)

I’ll be conservative and transparent with assumptions.

### For the employer (HR/ops/IT/legal combined)
Typical cross-border onboarding involves 20–60 “touches” (emails, checks, rework loops).  
If AI reduces rework and handoffs by even 40–60%:

- **Time saved:** ~6–15 staff hours per hire  
- If loaded cost is £35–£70/hr → **£210–£1,050 saved per hire**  
Bigger win: reduced start delays (even 3–7 days sooner) → material productivity impact.

### For the worker/contractor
- Less time chasing status + resubmitting documents  
- **Time saved:** ~2–6 hours per onboarding  
- Reduced anxiety + fewer missed start dates (economic protection)

### For EOR/payroll/compliance vendors (if integrated later)
- Cleaner intake, fewer exceptions → lower support cost per case

These numbers are “order-of-magnitude” because exact savings depend on company process maturity. But the mechanism is real: **exception handling is where time burns**, and AI is best at catching exceptions early.

- Spreadsheet checklists + email threads + “who owns this?” Slack ping loops

## Full scope (NO shrinking): lifecycle coverage

BorderOps AI covers **6 stages**:

# User stories + flows (detailed)

I’ll give you **8 user stories** (multiple user types) and *exactly how the system is used*.

---

## User story A — Founder/HR plans to hire globally (BEFORE job ad)

**Goal:** “Can I hire a React dev in Country X as employee vs contractor? What will break?”

**What exists today:** HR googles, asks payroll vendor, reads policy PDFs, waits days.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Global Hiring Planner**
2. Inputs:
   - Country = X
   - Role = React dev
   - Engagement = employee vs contractor
   - Company constraints (budget band, start date, security level)
3. Output (AI):
   - Recommended hiring route(s)
   - Compliance & payroll implications
   - Expected onboarding lead time
   - Required evidence list & risk flags
4. Action:
   - HR clicks “Generate Job Ad + Screening Rubric”

**Pain reduced:** removes “unknown unknowns” early.

---

## User story B — Recruiter/HR receives 300 CVs (sourcing stage)

**What exists today:** ATS keyword filters + manual review. ATS exists, but review time is still big. citeturn2search19turn2search1turn2search13

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Candidate Inbox**
2. Upload/Connect:
   - CVs / LinkedIn PDFs / portfolios
3. AI outputs per candidate:
   - Structured profile
   - Skill evidence map (projects ↔ skills)
   - Red flags (timeline gaps, inconsistency)
   - Fit score *within your rubric* (not generic)
4. HR selects shortlist → “Create interview plan”.

**Pain reduced:** faster triage + fewer bad interviews.

---

## User story C — Hiring manager runs interviews (multi-round)

**What exists today:** scheduling + notes scattered; feedback inconsistent.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Interview Kit**
2. AI generates:
   - Role-specific question bank
   - Structured scorecard
   - “Probe questions” based on candidate CV claims
3. After each round:
   - AI summarizes notes into structured evidence
   - Detects contradictions across rounds

**Pain reduced:** consistent interviewing, fewer false positives.

---

## User story D — Offer stage (contract + country rules)

**What exists today:** templates + legal reviews + vendor back-and-forth.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Offer Builder**
2. Inputs: salary, start date, location, engagement type
3. AI outputs:
   - Offer letter draft
   - Contract draft (clauses chosen based on country + company policy pack)
   - Risk report: what’s non-standard
4. Human legal approves final.

**Pain reduced:** legal time saved, fewer iterations.

---

## User story E — Worker onboarding (documents & identity)

**What exists today:** repeated uploads, unclear missing items.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Worker Portal**
2. Worker uploads:
   - ID, address proof, tax forms, bank info, certificates
3. AI:
   - Extracts fields
   - Validates consistency (name, DOB, address formats)
   - Flags missing/expired items
4. Worker sees a simple status:
   - “You are blocked by: 2 items”
   - “Next action: upload X”

**Pain reduced:** fewer resubmissions, clearer ownership.

---

## User story F — IT/security provisioning

**What exists today:** HR email → IT ticket → delays.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Access Readiness**
2. AI tells IT:
   - When to provision accounts
   - Minimum access set
   - Security checklist driven by role + geography
3. Auto-create tickets (Jira/ServiceNow later; in v1 we create internal tasks).

**Pain reduced:** faster start, fewer security mistakes.

---

## User story G — Timesheets & payments (post-hire ops)

**What exists today:** separate tools + manual checks + payment delays.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Timesheet & Pay Hub**
2. Worker submits hours
3. AI checks:
   - contract limits
   - unusual spikes
   - missing approvals
4. Finance exports to payroll vendor.

**Pain reduced:** fewer disputes + fewer payment delays.

---

## User story H — Drift monitoring (months later)

**What exists today:** nobody notices until failure.

**BorderOps flow:**
1. Screen: **Compliance Drift Monitor**
2. AI monitors:
   - document expiry
   - policy changes (company policy pack updates)
   - role/country changes
3. AI issues alerts:
   - “Right-to-work evidence expires in 30 days”
   - “Contract clause outdated for Country X policy version”

**Pain reduced:** prevents late-stage disruption.

---

# Screens (exact count) + navigation

### Employer/HR app — **10 screens**
1. Dashboard (portfolio overview)
2. Global Hiring Planner
3. Role Setup (rubric, requirements)
4. Candidate Inbox
5. Candidate Profile (evidence map)
6. Interview Kit
7. Decision Room (panel + rationale)
8. Offer Builder
9. Onboarding Orchestrator (task graph)
10. Timesheet & Pay Hub

### Worker app — **6 screens**
1. Worker Home (status)
2. Document Vault (upload + validation)
3. Tasks & Requests
4. Timesheet Submission
5. Payments Status
6. Notifications/Alerts

### Admin/Compliance — **4 screens**
1. Policy Pack Manager (company rules upload)
2. Country Rules Library (seed + overrides)
3. Audit Log Viewer
4. Model/Prompt Config (guardrails, templates)

**Total screens:** **20**

---

# Tech stack (specific, no hand-waving)

VisaVerse resources explicitly recommend OpenAI API, Hugging Face, React/Next.js, Node/Django/Flask, Firebase/Supabase, Postgres/MongoDB, cloud platforms. citeturn0search5  
So we pick a stack that is realistic and fast.

## Frontend
- **Next.js (React + TypeScript)**
- UI component library (shadcn/ui or MUI)
- Auth: **Clerk** or **Auth.js**

## Backend
- **Python FastAPI** (API + orchestration)
- Background jobs: **Celery + Redis** (or Temporal if you want heavier)

## Database (structured)
- **PostgreSQL** as source of truth
- Tables:
  - users, orgs, roles, candidates, interviews, offers, tasks, documents, payments, audit_logs
- Vector search:
  - **pgvector** (keep it in Postgres for simplicity)

## File storage (documents)
- **AWS S3** (or Cloudflare R2) for raw PDFs/images
- Encrypted at rest + signed URLs

## AI layer (specific models)
From OpenAI docs, current API model options include GPT-5.2 and GPT-4.1. citeturn1search2turn1search0  
So:

- **Primary reasoning model:** **GPT-5.2** (complex multi-step agent work) citeturn1search2  
- **High-throughput extraction model:** **GPT-4.1 mini** (fast doc parsing & structured output) citeturn1search2turn1search1  
- **Embeddings:** **text-embedding-3-large** (search + matching) citeturn1search2  
- **Moderation (if needed):** **omni-moderation** citeturn1search2  

(If you insist on open-weights too: add a Hugging Face reranker, but OpenAI stack alone is enough and explicitly allowed. citeturn0search5turn0search8)

## Observability
- **MLflow** for experiment tracking (explicitly recommended by VisaVerse) citeturn0search5  
- Logging: OpenTelemetry + PostHog/Sentry

---

# Data storage & privacy (explicit)

Where data is stored:
- **Postgres**: all structured records + audit trail
- **S3/R2**: raw documents
- **pgvector**: embeddings for semantic search on docs/candidates/policies

Retention controls:
- Org-level retention policy (30/90/365 days)
- “Delete candidate” = delete structured + documents + embeddings (hard delete)

---

# How much code / how many repos (concrete)

**1 monorepo** (fastest):
- `/apps/web` (Next.js)
- `/apps/api` (FastAPI)
- `/packages/shared` (schemas, types)
- `/infra` (docker-compose, terraform optional)

Code size reality (full-fledged v1):
- Web: ~80–150 components, ~20 pages/screens
- API: ~60–120 endpoints, ~20 background tasks
- DB: ~25–40 tables + migrations

---

## Why this is “full-fledged” and not a toy

Because it covers:
- Before hire (feasibility + job + screening)
- During hire (interviews + decision)
- After hire (offer + onboarding + IT + payments)
- Ongoing (drift monitoring)

And AI is not “summary”:
- It generates rubrics, probes, plans, dependency graphs, validation, contradiction checks.

---

Good question. This is **the right challenge**.  
I’ll answer it **cleanly, without defensiveness, without selling**, and by **contrasting with existing HR systems**—because if this can’t beat them conceptually, the product shouldn’t exist.

---

## 1️⃣ “Just by naming will it be borderless?” — **No. Name is irrelevant**

You’re right:  
Calling something *BorderOps* (or anything) **does not make it borderless**.

Borderless is **not a label**, it’s a **property of the system**.

So let’s remove the name entirely for this explanation.

---

## 2️⃣ “Why not just use existing HR platforms?” — THIS is the core question

You are right again:
- HR is one of the **oldest enterprise system categories**
- There are **hundreds of HR ERP / HRIS / ATS tools**
- If the pain still exists, either:
  - Everyone is incompetent (unlikely), or
  - The pain is **structural**, not implementation-level

Let’s examine this **precisely**.

---

## 3️⃣ What HR systems are ACTUALLY built for (fact)

Traditional HR systems evolved to solve **stable, internal problems**:

### What HR systems optimize for
- Employee records
- Payroll within a country
- Leave, attendance
- Performance reviews
- Compliance **inside one jurisdiction**
- Headcount reporting

They assume:
- One employer
- One country (or a few)
- One legal framework
- Long-term employment

This assumption is baked into their data models.

---

## 4️⃣ What changed in the real world (this is where the gap appears)

### The modern reality (post-2015, accelerated post-2020)
- Work is:
  - Cross-border
  - Contract-based
  - Platform-mediated
  - Remote-first
- One person can be:
  - Contractor in country A
  - Employee in country B
  - Freelancer on 2 platforms
- Companies hire:
  - Before entity setup
  - Via EOR
  - Via payroll partners
  - Via intermediaries

This **breaks HR assumptions**.

---

## 5️⃣ Why the pain STILL exists despite many HR tools

Because **no HR system owns the full problem**.

Let’s be explicit.

### HR tools are split into silos

| System | Owns | Does NOT own |
|---|---|---|
| ATS | Hiring pipeline | Legal feasibility |
| HRIS | Employee records | Pre-hire readiness |
| Payroll | Salary & tax | Hiring decision logic |
| EOR | Compliance execution | Talent evaluation |
| ITSM | Access provisioning | Legal/HR dependency logic |

Each tool works **locally**, not globally.

---

## 6️⃣ The REAL pain point (now stated precisely)

> **No system answers this question end-to-end:  
> “Can this person legally, operationally, and productively work for us in this role, in this country, starting on this date — and why?”**

HR systems answer **partial questions**:
- “Is the employee in our system?”
- “Is payroll configured?”
- “Is access requested?”

They do **not** answer:
- “What will block this *before* it breaks?”
- “What dependencies exist across teams?”
- “What happens when any variable changes?”

That’s why spreadsheets + emails still exist.

---

## 7️⃣ Why HR being “old” actually makes the gap worse

Older systems are:
- Schema-heavy
- Rule-based
- Deterministic
- Built for predictability

Cross-border work is:
- Contextual
- Exception-driven
- Policy-shifting
- Ambiguous

This mismatch is **not fixable by adding features** to HRIS.

---

## 8️⃣ So what is fundamentally DIFFERENT about this system?

### Key distinction (this matters)

**HR systems = record-keeping systems**  
**This system = reasoning system**

HR systems store:
- “What is true right now”

This system reasons:
- “What will become a problem if we proceed”
- “What depends on what”
- “What breaks if X changes”

That requires AI.

---

## 9️⃣ Why AI is mandatory here (not optional)

HR rules explode combinatorially when:
- Country × role × contract × policy × platform × time

Humans manage this with:
- Experience
- Gut feeling
- Escalation

AI manages this with:
- Dependency reasoning
- Document interpretation
- Conflict detection
- Scenario simulation

This is **not CRUD + chatbot**.

---

## 🔟 Why this pain persists EVEN in “good companies”

Because:
- The cost is **distributed** (HR, legal, IT each feel a piece)
- No single team owns “global operational readiness”
- Failures show up as:
  - Delays
  - Frustration
  - Silent inefficiency
(not a single red error)

Those are the hardest problems to kill.

---

## 11️⃣ Borderless ≠ replaces HR

Important correction:

This system is **not an HR replacement**.

It sits:
- **Across HR, Legal, IT, Payroll**
- Before, during, and after hire
- As a **coordination & reasoning layer**

Think of it as:
> “The system that explains why HR systems are stuck.”

---

## 12️⃣ Why THIS can exist alongside HR platforms

Because:
- HR platforms don’t want to own cross-border reasoning risk
- EOR vendors don’t want to own hiring logic
- Payroll vendors don’t want to own document truth
- IT doesn’t want to own legal dependencies

This gap is intentional — but painful.

---

## 13️⃣ Final clarity (no selling)

- ❌ It’s not borderless because of the name  
- ❌ It’s not better because HR tools are “bad”  
- ✅ It exists because **no system reasons across borders and silos**
- ✅ AI is required because rules are contextual and dynamic
- ✅ The pain persists because responsibility is fragmented

---

## One final grounding question (not rhetorical)

If tomorrow:
- HR tools disappeared,
- Payroll tools disappeared,
- EOR tools disappeared,

**Would a human still struggle to coordinate global hiring?**  
Yes — because the struggle is **reasoning**, not storage.

That’s the problem this system exists to solve.

Below is **one concrete scenario** and a **step-by-step walk** showing **exactly where HR tools stop** and **where this system starts**. I’ll keep it operational: who does what, what tools exist today, what breaks, and what AI does.

I’ll use a realistic pattern (no country-specific legal claims):  
**Hire as contractor in Country X → convert to employee in Country Y after 3 months**.

---

# Scenario setup

**Company:** UK-based remote company  
**Role:** Senior Data Engineer  
**Candidate:** Lives in **Country X** now, plans to relocate to **Country Y** in ~3 months  
**Goal:** Start work quickly as contractor in X, then convert to employee in Y with minimal disruption.

**Tools the company already has (typical):**
- ATS (e.g., Greenhouse/Lever) for hiring pipeline
- HRIS (e.g., Workday/BambooHR) for employee records
- Payroll/EOR vendor (e.g., Deel/Remote/Oyster) for cross-border employment/contracting
- IT ticketing (Jira/ServiceNow) for access/equipment
- Shared docs (Google Drive/Notion) for policies/templates

---

# Step-by-step: where HR tools stop vs where the system starts

## Step 1 — Requisition & plan (before job ad)
### What HR tools do today
- ATS stores the job requisition.
- HR may add “location: remote” and a note: “contractor first, then employee”.

✅ **HR tools can store the plan.**  
❌ They **do not** validate whether the plan is operationally coherent.

### Where the system starts
**System action (AI reasoning):** “Plan feasibility + dependency map”
- Inputs: role, start date target, current country X, future country Y, engagement plan (contractor→employee), internal policy pack (security level, equipment, data access constraints), preferred vendor (EOR).
- Output:
  1) **Two-track operational plan** (contractor track now; employee track later)
  2) **Dependency graph** with dates (what must be done before what)
  3) **Known friction points** (e.g., identity consistency, contract/role continuity, access and payroll changeover)
  4) **Evidence checklist** (what to collect once vs what differs by track)

**Why HR tools stop here:** ATS/HRIS don’t reason across payroll+IT+legal+time.

---

## Step 2 — CV screening and interviews
### What HR tools do today
- ATS manages resumes, interview stages, scorecards.
- Hiring manager collects feedback.

✅ This part is ATS-native.

### Where the system starts (optional but powerful)
**System action (AI):** “Claim consistency + evidence map”
- Reads CV + LinkedIn + portfolio + interview notes.
- Builds an “evidence map”: claimed skills ↔ proof artifacts.
- Flags likely future issues: ambiguous dates, overlapping employment, unclear client work (common contractor risk).

**Why it matters for later:** Contractor→employee conversions often trigger deeper checks; catching inconsistencies early prevents later churn.

---

## Step 3 — Decision: “Hire them”
### What HR tools do today
- ATS marks “hired”.
- HR sends offer / begins onboarding.

✅ ATS closes the loop.  
❌ No tool answers: “What is the fastest safe route to Day-1 productivity given the two-track plan?”

### Where the system starts
**System action (AI):** “Day-1 readiness plan”
- Produces:
  - Minimum viable access set for contractor start
  - Equipment/shipping plan (if any)
  - Compliance tasks split by owner (HR/worker/vendor/IT)
  - Timeline with “hard blockers vs soft blockers”

This is the first place you see **work starting** as a computed outcome, not a hopeful date.

---

## Step 4 — Contractor onboarding in Country X (start work)
### What exists today
- Vendor/EOR portal collects contractor agreement + bank info + tax form equivalents.
- HRIS may not store contractors properly, or stores them in a separate module.
- IT provisions accounts from a ticket request.
- Documents are scattered (Drive + vendor portal + HR email).

✅ Each system does its own intake.  
❌ Nobody maintains a single “truth state” of what’s done and what’s missing.

### Where the system starts (core)
**System action (AI):** “Evidence ingestion + validation”
- Worker uploads once (or forwards emails / connects sources).
- AI extracts structured facts (name, address, dates, identifiers, contract terms), and runs **cross-source consistency checks**.
- Produces a live status:
  - ✅ Done
  - ⚠ Needs review
  - ⛔ Blocking

**System action (AI):** “Exception killer”
- Detects issues that currently cause human ping-pong:
  - Name spelling mismatch across documents
  - Start date mismatch (offer vs vendor agreement)
  - Contract type mismatch (contractor vs employee wording)
  - Missing mandatory fields for vendor submission
- Suggests exact fix and who must do it.

**Where HR tools stop:** they record what you upload; they don’t reconcile and explain conflicts across systems.

---

## Step 5 — Work begins (Week 1–12 as contractor)
### What exists today
- Timesheets in vendor portal or separate time tool.
- HRIS record stale; IT access grows organically (“just grant them X”).
- No one prepares for conversion; it’s remembered late.

✅ Work happens.  
❌ The conversion is a future event that gets ignored until it’s urgent.

### Where the system starts
**System action (AI):** “Conversion runway manager”
- Starts a countdown from Day 1:
  - What must be collected continuously (proof of work, payroll history, performance artifacts, address changes)
  - What must be planned (employee contract, benefits, entity/EOR choice, access policy changes)
- Creates scheduled tasks for HR and worker with deadlines tied to planned move date.

**This is the “not one-off” part.** It’s continuous.

---

## Step 6 — Relocation trigger: “Moving to Country Y”
### What exists today
- Worker emails HR: “I’m moving on X date.”
- HR restarts discovery: “What do we need in Country Y?”
- Vendor proposes steps; IT re-checks security.

✅ People can do it manually.  
❌ It’s slow, repetitive, and fragile.

### Where the system starts (big)
**System action (AI):** “Scenario recomputation”
- Recomputes the entire dependency graph with new location and employee status:
  - Employee onboarding requirements in Y
  - Contract changes needed
  - Payroll setup requirements
  - IT/security policy changes (e.g., data access constraints)
- Outputs a conversion plan with:
  - Freeze dates (when contractor invoices stop)
  - Cutover date (employee start)
  - Parallel run plan (to avoid pay gaps / access loss)

**Where HR tools stop:** HRIS stores “location changed.” It does not compute the operational cutover.

---

## Step 7 — Employee onboarding in Country Y
### What exists today
- HRIS employee onboarding tasks (forms, benefits).
- Vendor collects employee documents.
- Legal reviews contract.
- IT changes access policies.

✅ Each tool covers its local tasks.  
❌ The conversion still risks:
- Pay gap
- Duplicate onboarding
- Conflicting records (contractor vs employee)
- Access over/under-provisioning

### Where the system starts
**System action (AI):** “Identity continuity + record merge”
- Ensures the worker is one identity across:
  - Contractor record(s)
  - Employee record
  - Vendor profile
  - IT accounts
- Flags anything that would cause duplicate records or compliance confusion.

**System action (AI):** “Cutover safety checks”
- Before the cutover date, AI verifies:
  - All blocking requirements satisfied
  - Contract aligned with role reality
  - Payroll readiness confirmed
  - IT access adjustments scheduled

This is a “release checklist”, but computed from real evidence, not a static list.

---

## Step 8 — After conversion: ongoing operations
### What exists today
- HRIS runs.
- Payroll runs.
- Vendor tickets happen.
- Drift is discovered late (expired docs, policy changes).

### Where the system starts
**System action (AI):** “Drift monitor”
- Watches:
  - Document expiries
  - Policy pack updates
  - Role changes
  - Cross-border travel events (if relevant)
- Alerts before it breaks payroll/access/compliance.

---

# The boundary in one line

**HR tools manage records and workflow inside their own silo.**  
**This system manages the cross-silo reasoning: dependencies, conflicts, cutovers, and continuous readiness.**

---

# What you should notice (why this isn’t “another HR platform”)

In this scenario, the system’s value appears in 4 moments where HR tools *cannot* help:

1) **Two-track planning (contractor now → employee later)**  
2) **Conflict detection across documents + systems**  
3) **Cutover computation (no pay/access gap)**  
4) **Continuous runway + drift monitoring**

That’s where human time is currently burned.

---

# EXACT “Where HR tools stop / system starts” in this scenario (pinpoint)

HR tools handle:
- ATS stages, interview scheduling, employee record storage, payroll execution inside vendor portals.

This system starts at:
1) **Two-track plan computation** (contractor now + employee later with dependencies)  
2) **Cross-document consistency** (identity continuity)  
3) **Cutover planning + safety checks** (no pay/access gap)  
4) **Drift monitoring** (things changing over months)

Those are not “features” of HRIS; they’re cross-silo reasoning.

# 1) Screen coverage validation (20 screens) + what I missed

### Your 20-screen list is correct. I used them, but I did NOT explicitly name the “import sources” UI for LinkedIn/portfolio.

**Fix (without adding a 21st screen):**  
The “Reads CV + LinkedIn + portfolio + interview notes” belongs inside existing screens as components:

- **Candidate Inbox (Screen 4)** includes **Import Sources panel**:
  - Upload CV PDF
  - Paste LinkedIn URL
  - Paste portfolio/GitHub URL
  - Upload work samples
- **Candidate Profile (Screen 5)** includes **Evidence Map tab**:
  - Skills ↔ evidence links from CV/LinkedIn/portfolio/interview notes
- **Interview Kit (Screen 6)** includes **Notes capture + structuring** (already specified)

So nothing is missing in screen count; the missing piece was **explicit placement** of source ingestion UI. Consider it corrected.

# 3) DB ENTITY LIST (tables + key fields)

This is the **minimum full-fledged schema** to persist every artifact above. PostgreSQL + pgvector assumed.

## Core org/auth

- id, org_id (FK), email, name, role (`HR|HM|IT|FIN|WORKER|ADMIN`), created_at

- user_id, org_id, role, status

### `hiring_plans`

- id, candidate_id, source_type (`cv_pdf|linkedin|portfolio|github|work_sample`), source_value, fetched_artifact_id, status

### `evidence_maps`
- id, candidate_id, version, evidence_map_json, created_at

- id, org_id, owner_type (`candidate|worker|org`), owner_id, doc_type, artifact_id, extracted_fields_json, validations_json, confidence, created_at

- id, owner_type, owner_id, conflicts_json, blocking, created_at

- id, worker_id, external_ref, status, created_at

# Scenario 2 (FULL DETAIL)
**Employee in Country Y from Day-1, but candidate is in Country X; remote start from X for 30 days; then relocation to Y.**

## What “Phase 2” means (clear definition)
**Phase 1 = Remote-Start Phase (Work from X, employee contract in Y, restricted access + provisional payroll readiness).**  
**Phase 2 = Local-Work Phase (After relocation to Y: address/tax/payroll finalization + access policy switch + steady-state).**

So “Phase 2” is not “contractor → employee”.  
It is **employee throughout**, but the **operational conditions change** after relocation.

---

# Global objects (IDs used consistently)
- `ORG_ID = ORG123`
- `PLAN_ID = PLAN_EMP_Y_REMOTE_X_001`
- `ROLE_ID = ROLE_SDE_001`
- `CAND_ID = CAND002`
- `WORKER_ID = WORK001` (candidate becomes worker after hire)
- Countries: `X` (current) → `Y` (employment country + future work location)
- Remote period: `30 days`
- Dates (example):
  - Offer accepted: `2026-01-05`
  - Start date (remote): `2026-01-20`
  - Planned relocation date: `2026-02-19`
  - Local phase begins: `2026-02-20`

## A17-ID — VALIDATION RULES (EXACT)

### Rule set `ID_V1`

| Rule ID | Rule | Failure Type |
|------|-----|-------------|
| ID_R1 | expiry_date > today | BLOCKING |
| ID_R2 | issuing_country != null | BLOCKING |
| ID_R3 | document_number length ≥ 6 | BLOCKING |
| ID_R4 | full_name contains ≥ 2 tokens | WARNING |
| ID_R5 | date_of_birth < today − 16 years | BLOCKING |

# CROSS-DOCUMENT CONSISTENCY (A18)

### Input
```json

  "docs":["DOC_ID_001","DOC_ADDR_X_001","DOC_TAX_Y_001"],
```

### Output
```json
{
  "conflicts":[
    {
      "type":"name_format_variation",
      "docs":["DOC_ID_001","DOC_ADDR_X_001"],
      "detail":"Uppercase vs title case",
      "severity":"low",
      "canonical_value":"RAJESH KUMAR S"
    }
  ],
  "blocking":false
}
```

### DB
- `doc_conflicts` INSERT
- `tasks` NOT blocked

---

# TASK STATE PROPAGATION (IMPORTANT)

If **any blocking rule fails**, system does:

### 1) Insert `doc_conflicts`
### 2) Update `tasks.status = blocked`
### 3) Create remediation task

Example:
```json
{
  "task_id":"T_FIX_ADDR",
  "owner_role":"Worker",
  "name":"Upload valid address proof (issued within 90 days)",
  "blocking":true
}
```

# PROJECT CANONICAL DEFINITION  
(ANTI-DRIFT / ANTI-AMNESIA BASELINE)

## Project Nature (what this is)

This project is a **cross-border work orchestration and reasoning system**.

It is **NOT**:
- an HRIS  
- an ATS  
- a payroll system  
- an EOR  
- a document storage app  
- a chatbot  

It **does not replace** those systems.

It exists **because those systems do not reason together**.

---

## The Core Problem (precise, not generic)

Modern work is **borderless, multi-phase, and event-driven**, but enterprise systems are:

- country-locked  
- phase-blind  
- siloed  
- record-keeping oriented  

As a result, **human coordination cost** explodes whenever:

- work starts in one country and continues in another  
- engagement type changes (contractor ↔ employee)  
- location changes mid-employment  
- access, payroll, and compliance must change **without disruption**  

Today, humans solve this with:
- spreadsheets  
- emails  
- Slack escalations  
- tribal knowledge  

This system exists to **replace that coordination burden with deterministic reasoning**.

---

## What the System Actually Does (operationally)

The system:

1. **Models work as phases**, not static states  
2. **Models dependencies** across HR, Legal, IT, Finance  
3. **Ingests evidence once** (CVs, IDs, addresses, tax forms, contracts)  
4. **Validates evidence deterministically**  
5. **Creates task graphs** instead of checklists  
6. **Recomputes requirements when reality changes**  
7. **Prevents pay gaps, access gaps, and compliance breaks**  
8. **Produces auditable reasoning trails**  

This is a **reasoning + orchestration layer**, not a CRUD system.

---

## The Core Design Rule (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

> **Nothing is assumed stable.**  
> Country, role, access, engagement, payroll, and policy can all change.

Therefore:
- Every step must be **stateful**
- Every state change must trigger **re-evaluation**
- Every decision must be **explainable from stored evidence**

---

## System Boundaries (clear)

### The system STARTS where:
- HR tools stop at “employee created”
- Payroll tools stop at “setup required”
- IT tools stop at “ticket created”
- EOR tools stop at “we’ll handle compliance”

### The system OWNS:
- sequencing  
- dependency reasoning  
- evidence consistency  
- phase transitions  
- cutovers  
- drift detection  

---

## Canonical Objects (mental model)

The system revolves around:

- **Plans** (intended future states)
- **Phases** (how work is allowed right now)
- **Evidence** (documents, facts, confirmations)
- **Tasks** (actions with dependencies)
- **Events** (things that change reality)
- **Rules** (policy + country constraints)
- **Decisions** (with justification)

If any future implementation removes one of these → it is a **broken system**.

---

# SUPPORTED SCENARIO SET (NOT JUST 2)

Below are the **MANDATORY scenarios** this system is designed to support.  
If a future page ignores these, it is **invalid**.

---

## SCENARIO GROUP A — ENTRY VARIANTS

### A1. Employee in Y, remote start from X → relocate to Y  
*(Scenario 2 already fully detailed)*

Pain:
- Payroll timing vs address proof
- Access restrictions vs productivity

---

### A2. Employee in Y, remote forever from X (no relocation)
Pain:
- Permanent cross-border access rules
- Tax exposure tracking
- Long-term compliance drift

---

### A3. Contractor in X, fast start → planned employee in Y  
*(Scenario 1 already detailed)*

Pain:
- Identity continuity
- Engagement conversion cutover
- No pay gap, no access reset

---

### A4. Contractor in X → contractor in Y  
Pain:
- Vendor change
- Contract replacement
- Tax & invoice continuity

---

## SCENARIO GROUP B — MID-LIFECYCLE CHANGES

### B1. Location change after 6 months (employee Y → Z)
Pain:
- Payroll restart
- Contract amendments
- Access policy switch

---

### B2. Role change (IC → lead) with higher access needs
Pain:
- Access escalation
- Policy violations if rushed
- Audit risk

---

### B3. Country rule change while employee is active
Pain:
- Silent non-compliance
- Late discovery penalties

---

### B4. Security incident forces immediate access downgrade
Pain:
- Access must change **without payroll or HR disruption**

---

## SCENARIO GROUP C — FAILURE / EXCEPTION HANDLING

### C1. Missing or invalid document blocks payroll
Pain:
- Worker angry
- HR scrambling
- Finance firefighting

System must:
- Detect early
- Assign clear remediation
- Preserve pay continuity where allowed

---

### C2. Name mismatch across ID / bank / tax docs
Pain:
- Vendor rejection
- Duplicate records

---

### C3. Worker relocates earlier or later than planned
Pain:
- Tasks mistimed
- Access wrong
- Payroll broken

---

### C4. Vendor lead time longer than expected
Pain:
- Start date at risk

---

## SCENARIO GROUP D — ORGANISATIONAL SCALE

### D1. Multiple hires across 5+ countries simultaneously
Pain:
- HR overwhelmed
- No unified view

---

### D2. Same worker has multiple engagements over time
Pain:
- Fragmented history
- Audit gaps

---

### D3. Policy update applies to only some workers
Pain:
- Selective remediation required

---

## SCENARIO GROUP E — GOVERNANCE & AUDIT

### E1. Internal audit asks: “Why was this allowed?”
System must answer with:
- Evidence
- Rules
- Decisions
- Dates

---

### E2. Regulator / client asks for proof of controls
System must:
- Produce traceable reasoning
- Not rely on human memory

---

# NON-FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS (DO NOT DROP)

- Deterministic validation (rules > vibes)
- Human override with audit trail
- No silent failures
- Every AI output persisted
- Every decision explainable later

---

# WHAT FUTURE PAGES ARE ALLOWED TO DO

Future pages may:
- Add new scenarios
- Add new document types
- Add new rules
- Add integrations

Future pages may **NOT**:
- Remove phases
- Collapse reasoning into “AI suggestions”
- Turn this into a chatbot
- Reduce it to “hackathon prototype thinking”

# I. REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION (FROZEN)

## Reference Scenario (RS-1) — **AUTHORITATIVE**

### **RS-1: Employee in Country Y, Remote Start from Country X (30 days), then Relocation to Y**

All future development MUST:
- work for RS-1
- be validated against RS-1
- not break RS-1

If something does not work for RS-1, it is **not acceptable**.

---

### Why RS-1 is chosen (not arbitrary)

RS-1 is the **highest-entropy baseline** because it includes:

- Employee engagement (not contractor shortcut)
- Country mismatch at start
- Time-bounded remote phase
- Phase transition without employment change
- Payroll, access, compliance, document dependency

If RS-1 works, **simpler scenarios are subsets**.

---

### RS-1 Mandatory Capabilities (Checklist)

RS-1 requires the system to support:

1. Multi-phase employment state (**same employee, different operational phases**)
2. Evidence reuse across phases
3. Time-dependent rules (30-day remote allowance)
4. Phase-specific access policies
5. Phase-specific document requirements
6. Payroll readiness decoupled from work start
7. Event-triggered recomputation (location change)
8. Zero pay gap
9. Zero access gap
10. Auditable reasoning for every transition

If any feature bypasses one of these → **reject it**.

---

# II. FAILURE-CASE WALKTHROUGHS (DETAILED, NOT ABSTRACT)

Below are **three failure scenarios** that MUST be supported **explicitly**, not implicitly.

---

## FAILURE CASE F-1  
### **Expired ID discovered after remote start but before payroll**

### Situation
- Worker started remote work (Phase-1)
- ID was valid at upload
- ID expires before first payroll run
- Payroll rules require valid ID at pay-run time

---

### What typically happens today
- Payroll rejects late
- HR scrambles
- Worker is unpaid or delayed
- No single system shows root cause

---

### REQUIRED SYSTEM BEHAVIOUR

#### Step 1 — Detection
- **A26 Drift Monitor** runs nightly
- Sees `government_id.expiry_date < payroll_cutoff_date`
- Generates **blocking alert**

```json
{
  "alert_type": "DOCUMENT_EXPIRY",
  "doc_id": "DOC_ID_001",
  "severity": "high",
  "blocking": true,
  "impact": "Payroll cannot proceed",
  "affected_phase": "phase_1_remote_start"
}
```

#### Step 2 — Task propagation
System must:
- Block payroll task
- Create remediation task

```json
{
  "task_id": "T_REUPLOAD_ID",
  "owner_role": "Worker",
  "name": "Upload renewed government ID",
  "blocking": true,
  "due_before": "2026-01-28"
}
```

#### Step 3 — Communication
- Worker notified (Screen 16)
- HR notified (Dashboard alert)
- Finance notified (Pay Hub warning)

#### Step 4 — Resolution
- Worker uploads new ID
- A17 validates
- A18 consistency check passes
- Payroll task unblocks automatically

**No human coordination required.**

## FAILURE CASE F-2  
### **Relocation delayed by 2 weeks (Phase-2 slips)**

### Situation
- Planned relocation: Feb 19
- Actual relocation: Mar 5
- Phase-2 tasks were scheduled based on old date

---

### What typically happens today
- Tasks fire too early
- Payroll setup wrong
- Access policies misaligned

---

### REQUIRED SYSTEM BEHAVIOUR

#### Step 1 — Event input
Worker updates status:
```json
{
  "event": {
    "type": "relocation_delay",
    "new_expected_date": "2026-03-05"
  }
}
```

#### Step 2 — Scenario recompute
**A24 — scenario_recompute_agent** must:

- Shift all phase-2 tasks forward
- Recalculate payroll cutoff
- Extend Phase-1 access rules
- Re-evaluate rule compliance

```json
{
  "changes": [
    {"task":"T6","old_due":"2026-02-19","new_due":"2026-03-05"},
    {"task":"T8","old_due":"2026-02-25","new_due":"2026-03-11"}
  ],
  "extended_phase": "phase_1_remote_start",
  "new_phase_2_start": "2026-03-06"
}
```

#### Step 3 — Safety checks
System must verify:
- Remote work still allowed under policy
- No rule violation caused by extension

If extension exceeds policy:
- Generate **policy exception task** (human review)

```json
{

  "canonical": "RAJESH KUMAR S",
  "variant": "Rajesh Kumar",
  "severity": "blocking"
}
```

#### Step 2 — Canonical resolution
System proposes fix:
- Canonical name = ID name
- Generate name-alignment instruction

#### Step 3 — Remediation task
```json
{
  "task_id": "T_FIX_TAX_NAME",
  "owner_role": "Worker",
  "name": "Re-submit tax form using canonical name",
  "blocking": true
}
```

#### Step 4 — Audit
Decision + resolution stored for future audits.

# III. PERFORMANCE & SCALE ASSUMPTIONS (EXPLICIT)

These are **design constraints**, not aspirations.

---

## Scale Targets (Initial)

| Dimension | Target |
|--------|-------|
| Organisations | 1 – 500 |
| Workers per org | 1 – 5,000 |
| Concurrent active workers | ~2,000 |
| Countries | 1 – 50 |
| Documents per worker | 5 – 30 |
| Tasks per worker lifecycle | 20 – 80 |

---

## Latency Expectations

| Operation | Acceptable |
|---------|-----------|
| Document extraction (A17) | ≤ 5 sec |
| Cross-doc consistency (A18) | ≤ 1 sec |
| Scenario recompute (A24) | ≤ 3 sec |
| Drift scan (A26) | batch, async |
| UI interaction | < 300 ms perceived |

---

## AI Usage Constraints

- AI calls are **not in hot UI paths** except:
  - A17 (doc upload)
  - A19 (“what next?”)
- All AI calls:
  - must be idempotent
  - must be persisted
  - must be replayable

---

## Failure Tolerance

- AI failure → task remains blocked, not guessed
- Retry with exponential backoff
- Human override always available (A32)

---

# IV. EXPLICIT NON-GOALS (SCOPE GUARDRAILS)

These are **hard exclusions** to prevent scope creep.

---

## The system will NOT:

1. Replace payroll vendors  
2. Calculate taxes or benefits  
3. Issue visas or immigration advice  
4. Act as legal authority  
5. Provide “AI recommendations” without evidence  
6. Automate hiring decisions  
7. Rank humans beyond defined rubrics  
8. Store biometric data  
9. Become a generic HRIS  
10. Become a chatbot interface for HR  

If a future idea pushes toward these → **reject it**.

---

## The system will ALWAYS:

- Require evidence
- Require rules
- Require explainability
- Allow human override
- Preserve audit trails

---

From this point onward:

- **RS-1 is the reference scenario**
- **Failure cases F-1, F-2, F-3 must be supported**
- **Performance & scale assumptions are fixed**
- **Non-goals are enforced**

Any future page must **reference this page explicitly** or it is invalid.

# 3️⃣ INTEGRATION BOUNDARIES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

## ATS (Greenhouse, Lever, etc.)

**We DO**
- Import candidate profile (name, CV, stage)
- Push decision outcome

**We DO NOT**
- Manage job postings
- Manage interview scheduling
- Replace ATS workflows

Boundary:
- ATS = hiring pipeline
- This system = readiness & reasoning

---

## Payroll / EOR (Deel, Remote, etc.)

**We DO**
- Prepare payroll-ready state
- Validate evidence before submission
- Track readiness + blockers

**We DO NOT**
- Calculate payroll
- Issue payments
- Act as employer of record

Boundary:
- Payroll executes
- This system prevents failure

---

## IT / IAM (Okta, Jira, ServiceNow)

**We DO**
- Recommend access tiers
- Track access state
- Enforce phase-based policies

**We DO NOT**
- Provision accounts directly (v1)
- Replace IAM

Boundary:
- IT executes
- This system reasons

---

# 4️⃣ DATA RETENTION & PRIVACY RULES

## Data classification

| Data | Class |
|----|------|
| ID docs | Highly sensitive |
| Address | Sensitive |
| Tax forms | Highly sensitive |
| CV / LinkedIn | Personal |
| Interview notes | Internal |

---

## Retention Rules

| Data | Retention |
|---|---|
| Candidate (not hired) | 90 days |
| Worker active | Employment + 7 years |
| Documents | Min required by law |
| AI runs | 1 year (audit) |

---

## Privacy Controls

- Per-org retention policy override
- Per-worker deletion request support
- Hard delete = documents + embeddings + AI logs

---

## AI Data Handling

- No training on customer data
- Prompts + outputs persisted for audit
- Redaction before human review

---

## Access Controls

- Role-based access
- Document access logged
- No cross-org visibility

---

# 🔷 PROJECT PAGE TEMPLATE — CANONICAL

## **PROJECT-CODE — SYSTEM BLUEPRINT & OPERATING BOUNDARIES**

### 1. Project Intent
- Why this project exists
- What problem space it addresses
- Why existing systems/tools are insufficient

---

### 2. What This System IS
- Core responsibilities
- Core abstractions
- What it reasons about
- What it orchestrates

---

### 3. What This System IS NOT
- Explicit exclusions
- Systems it does not replace
- Responsibilities it will never assume

---

### 4. Scenario Universe (MANDATORY)
List **all categories of scenarios**, not just one.

Example:
- Entry scenarios
- Mid-lifecycle changes
- Failure / exception cases
- Scale / organisation scenarios
- Governance / audit scenarios

(Scenarios can be added later — this list is living.)

---

### 5. Reference Scenario (Anchor, not a freeze)
- One scenario used as a **baseline anchor**
- Chosen because it has the highest complexity
- Other scenarios must not break this one

Language to use:
- “Reference”
- “Anchor”
- “Baseline”

❌ Avoid words like: freeze, lock, final

---

### 6. Failure & Exception Philosophy
- What happens when things go wrong
- How the system detects issues
- How remediation is handled
- What is automated vs human-reviewed

---

### 7. Operating Guardrails
- Performance expectations
- Scale assumptions
- Determinism requirements
- Explainability requirements

---

### 8. Integration Boundaries
For each external system:
- What we consume
- What we emit
- What we explicitly do not do

---

### 9. Data & Privacy Principles
- Data categories
- Retention philosophy
- Deletion philosophy
- AI usage constraints

---

### 10. Evolution Rules (VERY IMPORTANT)
- What can evolve freely
- What requires discussion
- What must never change silently

This section prevents future confusion more than anything else.

# A) HACK-003 (Attempt-2) — PAGE LIST (AUTHORITATIVE)

## **HACK-003-02 — SYSTEM BLUEPRINT & OPERATING BOUNDARIES** *(CURRENT PAGE)*
**Purpose:** Define what the system is/isn’t, scenarios, baseline reference scenario, failure handling, guardrails, integration boundaries, data/privacy rules.  
**Step-by-step (what happens here):**
1. Write “IS / IS NOT” boundaries  
2. List scenario universe (multiple categories)  
3. Pick 1 baseline reference scenario (RS-1)  
4. Write 2–3 failure walkthroughs (step-by-step)  
5. Define AI call catalog (A1–A32) + what triggers each  
6. Define DB entities (tables + key fields)  
7. Define API endpoints per screen + request/response contracts  
8. Define integration boundaries (ATS, Payroll, IT)  
9. Define data retention + privacy rules  
**Will NOT happen here:** coding, UI polishing, sprint boards, demo video writing.  
**Output to close page:** “Blueprint Complete” checklist ticked with links/sections to each item above.

---

## **HACK-003-02A — ARCHITECTURE & INTERFACE SPEC (BUILD-READY)**
**Purpose:** Convert blueprint into buildable architecture + interface design, without coding.  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Choose deployment style (monolith vs services) and justify  
2. Finalize tech stack (frontend, backend, DB, queue, storage)  
3. Create system diagram: UI → API → AI service → DB → integrations  
4. Finalize authentication & roles (RBAC matrix)  
5. Write OpenAPI spec skeleton (paths, schemas, auth)  
6. Finalize DB schema (types, indexes, constraints, pgvector usage)  
7. Define async jobs (drift scan, document recheck) and queues  
8. Define observability (logs, audit, metrics)  
**Will NOT happen:** implementing endpoints, writing frontend components.  
**Output to close page:** Architecture diagram + OpenAPI v1 + DB DDL v1 + RBAC matrix.

---

## **HACK-003-02B — UX FLOW & SCREEN-BY-SCREEN WIREFRAME SPEC**
**Purpose:** Lock navigation + flows per screen so build doesn’t drift.  
**Step-by-step:**
1. List screens (20) + navigation map  
2. For each screen: components, buttons, forms, error states  
3. For each button: exact API call + success/fail UI response  
4. For RS-1: walk full click-path (already started) and confirm no gaps  
5. Define notification rules (who gets what alert, when)  
**Will NOT happen:** visual branding perfection, marketing copy.  
**Output to close page:** Screen spec sheets + nav map + RS-1 click-path finalized.

---

## **HACK-003-02C — BUILD PLAN & SPRINT BOARD (NO CODE)**
**Purpose:** Plan execution like a real IT project (sprints, tasks, owners).  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Define sprint length (e.g., 1 week) and total sprints available until deadline  
2. Break work into epics: Auth, Plans, Candidate, Docs, Tasks, Access, Pay, Drift, Audit  
3. Define Definition of Done per epic  
4. Create backlog with priority order aligned to RS-1  
5. Identify risks & mitigations (integration unknowns, doc parsing edge cases)  
**Will NOT happen:** implementing features.  
**Output to close page:** Sprint backlog + epic checklist + DoD list.

---

## **HACK-003-02D — IMPLEMENTATION (CODE TRACKER)**
**Purpose:** Coding work page — the only place where we write/track code changes.  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Repo structure + environment setup  
2. Implement endpoints in order of RS-1 flow  
3. Implement DB migrations + seed data  
4. Implement AI service wrappers for A1–A32  
5. Implement UI screens in navigation order  
6. Run RS-1 manually end-to-end  
**Will NOT happen:** redefining scope; blueprint arguments.  
**Output to close page:** Running app + README run steps + demo script working locally.

---

## **HACK-003-02E — TESTING (AUTOMATED + FAILURE TESTS)**
**Purpose:** Validate behaviour with tests (not redesign).  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Write automated tests per AI call (unit tests using fixtures)  
2. Write API contract tests (request/response schema)  
3. Write RS-1 end-to-end tests (playwright/cypress or API-only)  
4. Write failure-case tests: F-1/F-2/F-3 walkthroughs  
5. Add test report output and “known limitations” list  
**Will NOT happen:** new features, new flows.  
**Output to close page:** Test suite runs green + report summary.

---

## **HACK-003-02F — DEMO & SUBMISSION PACKAGE**
**Purpose:** Devpost-ready: demo video, write-up, repo polish, diagrams, disclaimers.  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Write demo script (2–5 min) using RS-1 + 1 failure case  
2. Record screen walkthrough  
3. Create Devpost description sections  
4. Finalize README, setup, architecture diagram  
5. “AI use” disclosure: what calls exist, what data is stored, what is non-goal  
**Will NOT happen:** building new capabilities.  
**Output to close page:** Devpost submission complete + final repo link + video link.

---

## **HACK-003-02G — OPERATIONS & NEXT ITERATION (POST-SUBMISSION)**
**Purpose:** After submission: feedback, issues, roadmap, but not mixed into build pages.  
**Step-by-step:**
1. Collect issues from demo/test  
2. Map sponsor feedback / judge feedback  
3. Roadmap and refactor plan  
**Will NOT happen:** rewriting the submission narrative.  
**Output to close page:** backlog for iteration v2.

---

## My honest take **after reading the full project gallery**

### 1️⃣ The gallery proves one thing clearly  
**VisaVerse is NOT a “visa hackathon”.**

Despite the branding, most submissions fall into these buckets:

1. **Generic AI assistants / copilots**  
   - Visa copilots  
   - Travel assistants  
   - Document helpers  
   - Eligibility chatbots  

2. **Unrelated-but-allowed AI projects**  
   - Healthcare  
   - Cybersecurity  
   - Retail orchestration  
   - Market research simulators  
   - IDE tools  

3. **Big-claim “OS / Protocol / Platform” entries**  
   - “VisaVerse OS”  
   - “Global mobility protocol”  
   - “AI identity passport”  
   - “Fraud defense layer”  

Judges are already seeing **dozens** of:
- “AI that helps with visa documents”
- “AI that answers eligibility questions”
- “AI chatbot for migrants”

👉 **That space is saturated.**

---

### 2️⃣ Most projects are shallow in a *specific way*  
This is the important insight.

Even the “big-sounding” projects usually:
- Stop at **advice**
- Stop at **chat**
- Stop at **single-user flows**
- Do **not** model lifecycle, state, or failure

They say:
> “We help users apply better”

They do **not** say:
> “We prevent operational failure when reality changes mid-process”

There is almost **no serious treatment of**:
- Multi-phase work
- State transitions
- Evidence continuity
- Cross-system orchestration
- Failure recovery
- Auditability

This is the **gap**.

---

### 3️⃣ Where your project sits relative to the gallery

Your project is **not competing** with:
- Visa copilots
- Eligibility bots
- Document summarizers

It competes with the **top 5–10%** that *claim* to be:
- Platforms
- OS layers
- Protocols
- Infrastructure for borderless work

But here’s the key difference:

#### Those projects mostly **claim breadth**
You are defining **depth**.

They say:
> “End-to-end AI platform for global mobility”

You are saying:
> “Here is the exact operational machinery that prevents payroll, access, and compliance from breaking when work crosses borders.”

That’s a **qualitatively different category**.

---

### 4️⃣ Why this is *not* a toy — relative to the gallery

Based on the gallery:

- A “toy” in this hackathon =  
  *single-user, single-flow, chat-first, no failure handling*

- Your project:
  - Models **state**
  - Models **time**
  - Models **phases**
  - Models **dependencies**
  - Models **failure**
  - Models **audit**

That already puts it above **80–90%** of submissions in **technical seriousness**, even if the UI is basic.

---

### 5️⃣ The risk (this is important)

The **only real risk** for your project in this hackathon is **misinterpretation**.

If judges see it as:
- “HR tool”
- “Onboarding software”
- “Enterprise workflow system”

They may think:
> “This is heavy / niche / enterprise-y”

So the danger is **not scope** —  
the danger is **positioning clarity**.

---

### 6️⃣ How judges are likely to read it (based on gallery patterns)

Judges are mentally sorting projects into:

- “Another copilot”
- “Interesting idea but shallow”
- “Ambitious but vague”
- **“This one actually thinks through reality”**

Your project falls into the **last category** *if*:

- You show **one concrete scenario**
- You show **what breaks today**
- You show **how your system prevents breakage**
- You show **failure recovery**, not just success

That’s why RS-1 + 1 failure case in the demo matters more than breadth.

---

### 7️⃣ My blunt conclusion

After reading the gallery:

- ❌ Building a visa chatbot would be a **losing move**
- ❌ Shrinking to “hack demo” would be a **self-own**
- ❌ Competing on UI polish is pointless

- ✅ A **reasoning + orchestration layer** for borderless work is:
  - Rare in the gallery
  - Memorable to judges
  - Clearly differentiated
  - Aligned with “global mobility” in a *non-trivial* way

Your current direction is **one of the very few that is defensible** *if* you stay disciplined and don’t let it collapse into “assistant mode”.

---

### 8️⃣ One sentence verdict

> **Compared to the VisaVerse gallery, this project is not a toy — it’s one of the few entries that treats global mobility as an operational system instead of a chat problem.**

If you want, next we can:
- Stress-test this positioning against **top 3 gallery competitors**
- Rewrite the project **one-liner** so judges don’t misclassify it
- Decide **exactly what NOT to show** in the demo to avoid confusion

# 🎯 WHAT VisaVerse Atlas IS (ONE SENTENCE)

**VisaVerse Atlas is an AI-driven operational orchestration system that ensures cross-border work never breaks payroll, access, or compliance when reality changes.**

Not advice.  
Not chat.  
Not documents.  
**Operational correctness.**

---

# 🧱 CORE PRINCIPLE (THIS IS THE CONTRACT)

---

# 1️⃣ REQUIREMENTS (AUTHORITATIVE)

Each requirement has an ID.  
If later code doesn’t satisfy it → **fail**.

---

## R-01 — Cross-Border Work Must Be Phase-Aware
**Requirement**  
The system MUST model work as **phases**, not a single static state.

**Why this exists**  
Real work starts remotely, relocates, changes access, changes payroll timing.

**Success condition**
- System knows whether worker is in Phase-1 (remote) or Phase-2 (local)

---

## R-02 — No Pay Gap Guarantee
**Requirement**  
The system MUST prevent payroll execution unless all required evidence is valid.

**Why**
Payroll failures destroy trust and cannot be fixed later.

---

## R-03 — No Access Gap Guarantee
**Requirement**  
The system MUST provision access appropriate to the current phase.

**Why**
Over-access = security breach  
Under-access = productivity failure

---

## R-04 — Evidence Must Be Deterministic
**Requirement**  
Documents MUST be extracted, validated, and re-validated deterministically.

**Why**
“Looks OK” is not compliance.

---

## R-05 — Reality Changes Must Trigger Re-Evaluation
**Requirement**  
Any change (location, date, engagement) MUST recompute requirements.

**Why**
Static workflows fail in the real world.

---

## R-06 — Failure Must Be Visible and Recoverable
**Requirement**  
When something breaks, the system MUST:
- detect early
- block downstream actions
- create a remediation path

---

## R-07 — Full Auditability
**Requirement**  
Every decision MUST be explainable later:
- what rule
- what evidence
- what time

---

# 2️⃣ USE CASES (WHAT USERS ACTUALLY DO)

---

## UC-01 — Employer Starts Cross-Border Hire
**Actor:** Employer / HR  
**Goal:** Start work without future failure

**Maps to:** R-01, R-02, R-03

---

## UC-02 — Worker Uploads Documents
**Actor:** Worker  
**Goal:** Become payroll & access ready

**Maps to:** R-04

---

## UC-03 — Worker Relocates / Changes State
**Actor:** Worker  
**Goal:** Continue working without break

**Maps to:** R-05

---

## UC-04 — Finance Runs Payroll
**Actor:** Finance  
**Goal:** Pay only when safe

**Maps to:** R-02, R-07

---

## UC-05 — IT Provisions Access
**Actor:** IT  
**Goal:** Right access, right time

**Maps to:** R-03, R-07

---

# 3️⃣ SCENARIOS (REALITY, NOT DIAGRAMS)

## SC-01 (REFERENCE SCENARIO)
**Employee in Country Y, remote start from Country X, relocate after 30 days**

### Phases
- Phase-1: Remote (X)
- Phase-2: Local (Y)

### What must NOT break
- Payroll
- Access
- Compliance

---

## SC-02 (FAILURE)
**ID expires after start but before payroll**

Expected:
- Payroll blocked
- Worker alerted
- Remediation task created

---

## SC-03 (FAILURE)
**Relocation delayed by 2 weeks**

Expected:
- Phase-2 tasks rescheduled
- Phase-1 access extended
- No silent violation

---

## Backend
- **Language:** Python 3.11
- **Framework:** FastAPI
- **Reason:** async, explicit schemas, testable

---

## Database
- **Primary DB:** PostgreSQL 15
- **ORM:** SQLAlchemy
- **Migrations:** Alembic

---

## Storage
- **Documents:** S3-compatible object store
- **Metadata:** PostgreSQL JSONB

---

- **LLM:** OpenAI GPT-4-class model (via API)
- **OCR:** Tesseract / Vision API (abstracted)
- **Rule engine:** Deterministic Python logic (NOT LLM)

---

## Frontend
- **Framework:** React + TypeScript
- **Reason:** explicit typing, testable flows

---

## Auth
- **Method:** JWT
- **Roles:** Employer, Worker, Admin, Finance, IT

Product name

Name: BorderOps AI
Why this name (brutal):

Not tied to the hackathon name (avoids the mistake you flagged).

Describes what it actually does: border + operations (not “visa”, not “docs”, not “chatbot”).

Broad enough to cover: hiring → onboarding → compliance → work start → timesheets → payroll → renewals.
Why this product (and why it’s a real pain point)
The real pain

Cross-border hiring & work fails because the workflow is fragmented across:

ATS/recruiting tools (job posts, CVs, interviews)

HRIS/onboarding tasks

EOR/payroll vendors

IT provisioning/security

Compliance/legal/policy docs

Even good platforms exist (ATS like Greenhouse/Lever; global payroll/EOR like Deel/Remote/Oyster; HR platforms like Rippling), but the end-to-end journey still becomes spreadsheet + email glue when borders and rules change. (Greenhouse)

What people do today (workaround)

Spreadsheet checklists + email threads + “who owns this?” Slack ping loops

Duplicate data entry across ATS → HRIS → payroll vendor

Late discovery of blockers (contract clause, ID mismatch, tax form gaps, access delays)

That’s why it’s a pain point: coordination cost + exception handling.

Full scope (NO shrinking): lifecycle coverage

BorderOps AI covers 6 stages:

Plan role & country feasibility (before job ad)

Sourcing + CV triage

Interview loops + decision

Offer + contract generation + risk checks

Onboarding execution (HR/Legal/IT/Worker)

Post-hire ops: timesheets, payments, renewals, drift monitoring

This directly matches VisaVerse themes like international workforce tools, onboarding, collaboration, compliance automation. (VisaVerse AI Hackathon)

User stories + flows (detailed)

I’ll give you 8 user stories (multiple user types) and exactly how the system is used.

User story A — Founder/HR plans to hire globally (BEFORE job ad)

Goal: “Can I hire a React dev in Country X as employee vs contractor? What will break?”

What exists today: HR googles, asks payroll vendor, reads policy PDFs, waits days.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Global Hiring Planner

Inputs:

Country = X

Role = React dev

Engagement = employee vs contractor

Company constraints (budget band, start date, security level)

Output (AI):

Recommended hiring route(s)

Compliance & payroll implications

Expected onboarding lead time

Required evidence list & risk flags

Action:

HR clicks “Generate Job Ad + Screening Rubric”

Pain reduced: removes “unknown unknowns” early.

User story B — Recruiter/HR receives 300 CVs (sourcing stage)

What exists today: ATS keyword filters + manual review. ATS exists, but review time is still big. (Oleeo)

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Candidate Inbox

Upload/Connect:

CVs / LinkedIn PDFs / portfolios

AI outputs per candidate:

Structured profile

Skill evidence map (projects ↔ skills)

Red flags (timeline gaps, inconsistency)

Fit score within your rubric (not generic)

HR selects shortlist → “Create interview plan”.

Pain reduced: faster triage + fewer bad interviews.

User story C — Hiring manager runs interviews (multi-round)

What exists today: scheduling + notes scattered; feedback inconsistent.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Interview Kit

AI generates:

Role-specific question bank

Structured scorecard

“Probe questions” based on candidate CV claims

After each round:

AI summarizes notes into structured evidence

Detects contradictions across rounds

Pain reduced: consistent interviewing, fewer false positives.

User story D — Offer stage (contract + country rules)

What exists today: templates + legal reviews + vendor back-and-forth.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Offer Builder

Inputs: salary, start date, location, engagement type

AI outputs:

Offer letter draft

Contract draft (clauses chosen based on country + company policy pack)

Risk report: what’s non-standard

Human legal approves final.

Pain reduced: legal time saved, fewer iterations.

User story E — Worker onboarding (documents & identity)

What exists today: repeated uploads, unclear missing items.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Worker Portal

Worker uploads:

ID, address proof, tax forms, bank info, certificates

AI:

Extracts fields

Validates consistency (name, DOB, address formats)

Flags missing/expired items

Worker sees a simple status:

“You are blocked by: 2 items”

“Next action: upload X”

Pain reduced: fewer resubmissions, clearer ownership.

User story F — IT/security provisioning

What exists today: HR email → IT ticket → delays.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Access Readiness

AI tells IT:

When to provision accounts

Minimum access set

Security checklist driven by role + geography

Auto-create tickets (Jira/ServiceNow later; in v1 we create internal tasks).

Pain reduced: faster start, fewer security mistakes.

User story G — Timesheets & payments (post-hire ops)

What exists today: separate tools + manual checks + payment delays.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Timesheet & Pay Hub

Worker submits hours

AI checks:

contract limits

unusual spikes

missing approvals

Finance exports to payroll vendor.

Pain reduced: fewer disputes + fewer payment delays.

User story H — Drift monitoring (months later)

What exists today: nobody notices until failure.

BorderOps flow:

Screen: Compliance Drift Monitor

AI monitors:

document expiry

policy changes (company policy pack updates)

role/country changes

AI issues alerts:

“Right-to-work evidence expires in 30 days”

“Contract clause outdated for Country X policy version”

Pain reduced: prevents late-stage disruption.
